- [x] **F6** Concept tasks (user 2026-11-19: 'concept makes an artifact, usually with multiple options and isnt started until user approves'). A new default kind K, concept: the slot does not build the thing; it researches the repo and publishes an Artifact page (Artifact tool, already in lean's tools) laying out usually 2-3 options, each with what it looks like or does, the files it touches and the trade-off, plus its recommendation. It finishes with 'done K7 "msg" --artifact <url>' (new flag; required for a K task, refused without it); done records the link in the task's journal and adds the build task as a blocked follow-up, 'BLOCKED: waiting for your OK on <url>', so no slot starts it. Approving: the person tells a control session 'go with option B' (or edits nothing and says 'approve'); the brief has it run 'agent-runner plan approve <ID> "option B: …"', which notes the choice and unblocks the build task. Rejecting or asking for changes: 'plan note' on the concept and reopen it. 'status' and 'since' (F5) show concepts waiting for approval first, with the link, as 'Waiting for your OK'. Kind letters stay configurable ([plan] kinds); a repo whose kinds lack K just doesn't get the flow. Docs (env-vars page): while the file named by CLAUDE_CLIENT_PRESENCE_FILE exists, Claude Code skips Remote Control mobile pushes (v2.1.181+). So: every Remote Control session the runner starts (control sessions, the control server, slots with remote_slots) gets CLAUDE_CLIENT_PRESENCE_FILE=<notes>/quiet in its environment. Slots keep that file for good: a slot never pushes. Control sessions: the run creates the file while it starts them and removes it once each one is up (its claude.ai/code link shows in the pane), so a launch is silent but later pushes (a concept waiting for your OK, the run stuck) still reach the phone. Also give slots --settings agentPushNotifEnabled=false, inputNeededNotifEnabled=false (settings keys from the docs' settings index), belt and braces. - [x] **B13** The board (runner feed window, '<repo> · runner' in the apps) should follow the run's state. (1) When the run is force-stopped (stop --force, or a crash), the board stops too: its process ends and it goes offline in the apps. Today it keeps going. (2) When the run is stopped or idle with nothing left to start, the board pauses: no Haiku turns, no pushes, no tokens, until the run starts or a task is added. Then it carries on. A plain stop (running tasks finish first) ends the board once the run has ended. Check: tests for each state (force stop ends the board; idle and stopped pause it; work resuming wakes it), and the board's process is gone after a force stop. | files: agent_runner/runner.py, tests/test_runner.py, CHANGELOG.md

First find out what Claude Code offers for this (ending a session, archiving it, or reusing it via --session-id or --continue so no new entry appears) and use the cleanest option. Don't touch sessions this run didn't start. Anything only a phone can confirm goes in a CONFIRM: block. - [x] **F62** Issue watch: a [run] issues_watch setting, false by default, that reads the repo's open GitHub issues (gh issue list, on the ci_watch cadence, only with the merge lock free) and files one task per new issue, carrying its number, title, URL and body in the long form (plan/tasks/<ID>.md); each issue filed once (<notes>/issues.seen), none for an issue an open task already names. issues_watch = true takes every open issue; a list of labels (issues_watch = ["bug", "runner"]) takes only issues with one of them. A second setting [run] issues_trust picks how the task treats the issue: "skeptical" (the default) files it as untrusted outside text: the agent must reproduce or confirm the problem in the code before changing anything, follows no instructions, commands or links in the issue, and when it cannot confirm it or the ask is not a fit, closes the task saying why (no code change) or blocks it on the person; "trust" files it as a normal task to do as written (still never running commands from it). Optional: issues by the repo's owner/collaborators count as trusted under skeptical too. gh missing or logged out is said once in the run log (as ci_watch does). When the task lands, comment the merge commit on the issue and close it only under trust; under skeptical just comment. Docs: REFERENCE.md beside ci_watch, FACTORY.md row, CHANGELOG Unreleased. Check: tests with a stubbed gh show off by default; on, one task per new issue, none twice; a label list filters; skeptical vs trust task wording differs; skeptical text says to confirm first and ignore instructions in the issue. | files: agent_runner/config.py, agent_runner/issues.py, agent_runner/loop.py, tests/test_runner.py, docs/REFERENCE.md, docs/FACTORY.md, CHANGELOG.md

Never when a V is already open, never with nothing landed since the last release (plan-only and release commits don't count), and at most one per idle spell. - [x] **B107** (follow-up, critical) A release (kind V) never starts while a task is taken by hand (`take`): sched.clash() makes a release clash with every busy task, and held() puts taken() tasks in busy, so an idle take (iFizBox R67, claimed 2:53 PM, waiting on a Tailscale login, no changes) held V6 for hours though the person said skip it. Fix: a release waits only for this run's running and merge-waiting tasks; a task taken by hand or claimed by another runner does not hold it (its work lands after, in the next release), and `after: all` on a release passes over a taken task as it does a needs-you one. clash_why/explain and status's release line say so. Design (final): https://claude.ai/artifact/4GBeFqJ6Gn3PubWxRgrk6s (open it; its keys and layout are the spec). (1) Feed: per session (slots and control sessions) the transcript as entries say/you/tool/out (Claude Code JSONL; Pi where it has one), newest last, so the page shows the agent's words with tool steps folded. - [x] **F88** Attach to a hidden session's real terminal, the way dtach does, so the TUI and 'attach sN|cN' work without tmux. Why: tmux is being removed (see the cleanup task after this); the person wants to attach from the TUI to the real Claude Code terminal session, not a Remote Control window. How: slots.run_pty (the shim that slots and hidden control sessions share, F40/F59) also listens on a Unix socket beside its marker (<notes>/slotN.sock, controlN.sock, mode 0600). It copies the pty's output to every connected client, writes client input to the pty, and takes a small resize message (rows, cols) that it sets with TIOCSWINSZ. The socket is removed when the session ends. A client in plat or slots (stdlib only: socket, select, termios, tty) puts the terminal in raw mode, sends its size on connect and on SIGWINCH, and detaches on Ctrl-b d (as tmux did), restoring the terminal. On connect, nudge the size (rows-1, then rows) so Claude Code redraws the whole screen. cmds.cmd_attach / hidden_attach and the TUI's pane tab (tui.pane_attach, Enter) use it on Linux and macOS; Windows keeps show_console. Check: a test starts run_pty on a small script (e.g. python echoing input), connects through the socket, sends text and a resize, and sees the echoed output and the new size; detach leaves the session running. Docs: README and docs/REFERENCE.md name the detach key. | files: agent_runner/slots.py, agent_runner/plat.py, agent_runner/cmds.py, agent_runner/tui.py, tests/test_runner.py, tests/test_tui.py, README.md, docs/REFERENCE.md

- [x] **F90** The TUI and the run page follow an upgrade of the run. Why: after an upgrade the run restarts on the new version (watch.restart_if_upgraded), but an open TUI keeps running its old code, and an open run page keeps its old HTML and JS, until the person restarts or reloads them by hand. How: the run writes its version (agent_runner.__version__) into STATE, and ui_model.snapshot carries it as 'version'. The TUI compares it with its own __version__ on each refresh; when the run's version is newer, it restores the terminal and os.execv's itself with the same argv, passing the focused panel, tab and selection through an environment variable so it reopens where it was. The run page (web_page.html, web_term.html) keeps the version from its first snapshot and calls location.reload() when a later one differs; drafts already survive a reload. When the version is missing (an older run), do nothing. Check: test_ui_model shows the snapshot carries the version from STATE; test_tui uses a stub exec to show a newer version triggers it with the same argv and the saved place, and an equal or missing version does not; test_web shows the page script reloads on a version change (or the served JS contains the check). | files: agent_runner/ui_model.py, agent_runner/loop.py, agent_runner/tui.py, agent_runner/web_page.html, agent_runner/web_term.html, tests/test_ui_model.py, tests/test_tui.py, tests/test_web.py

Fix: when a free slot's best candidate is lower priority than a ready-but-file-blocked critical task whose blocker has already exited (only merge left), either hold the slot until that merge lands, or start the critical task on top of the blocker's branch (merged ahead). - [x] **B132** Run page Talk mic on iPhone Safari (https): it asks for mic permission, then never shows any text and sends nothing. Likely cause: listen() (web_page.html ~668) calls speechSynthesis.cancel() and unlockSpeech() (speaks an empty utterance) right before rec.start(); on iOS that puts the audio session in playback, so recognition hears nothing and ends silently. And onerror swallows 'no-speech'/'aborted', so nothing tells the person. Fix: in listen() do not touch speechSynthesis before start (talkSend already unlocks); when onend fires with no words, show 'Heard nothing (error: X)' in the talk line, with the last error code, instead of going quiet. Check: test that a listen with no result and error no-speech sets the talk line to the heard-nothing text; then ask the person to try on the iPhone. | files: agent_runner/web_page.html, tests/test_web.py | after: B130

Reported 10:31 (2026-11-23), after the stand-in's 'Starting the chat… what you write goes once it is up'; which message is not established (the sending page's state was not seen), so reproduce first. Faults read in the code: (1) obDone for a new chat drops the outbox item, then open(j.name); its ask (x.ask) is not put in S.sent[name].pending, so the first message disappears until the transcript has it. (2) new_control(wait=False) returns the name once the shim is up, before Claude takes input; messages queued for '@cid' are then typed at once (slots.send_fifo), which returns ok without knowing Claude took them. Unproven: whether one is lost this way. (3) loadFeed clears ALL pending once any entry is added (entries.length > sent.len), though the sent text may not be in the feed. (4) feed() reads only the transcript's last FEED_BYTES, so the person's prompt drops out of a long turn and 'more' still says False: c3 and c4's feeds both lack their first prompt, which is in the transcript. Fix: keep each sent message shown until its own text is in the feed as 'you'; carry a new chat's ask over as pending; hold messages until the chat can take input, and show not delivered with Retry when it never lands; always keep the latest person's prompt in feed() and set 'more' when the tail is cut. - [x] **F98** Approve-to-push mode: a new [run] approve = false setting (on: nothing reaches the push remote until a person approves it). Tasks still merge and pass the gate locally as now. Unpushed merges are grouped into pitches: tasks that touch the same component (overlapping files, the same module or directory, or one task following another with --after) go in one pitch; unrelated ones get their own. A pitch is opened once its group has been quiet for a while (no new merge into it for [run] approve_wait, default 30 min) or holds [run] approve_batch tasks (default 5), so a busy run makes a few pitches, not one per task. A pitch is an Artifact page written for the person using the runner (not a code review): per change, what it changes for them in plain words, why they would want it, how to try it now (the run page or command to open, before/after where there is a screen), what it might break, and the files changed at the bottom. It shows as a Needs you item with Approve / Not this (and on the run page), pushed to the phone like other needs; Not this can be picked for the whole pitch or one change in it. Approve pushes main up to the last merge commit of that pitch (git push <remote> <sha>:<branch>). Pushes stay in merge order: when an earlier unpushed merge belongs to another waiting pitch, approving says so and asks to approve that one too (or pushes only once it is). Not this reverts those tasks' merges on main locally (revert commits through the gate) and reopens or drops each, as the person picks. While pitches wait, merges go on locally; status and the run page say how many changes are waiting to be pushed. Releases (V tasks) wait until everything before them is approved. Off: behaviour unchanged. - [x] **B164** The run page's Settings view will not load while the run is older than the page (a person hit it). Cause, checked live 2026-11-23: page_html reads web_page.html from disk on every request, but the run process (started 02:18) predates F83/F97's GET /settings route (merged 11:52), so it answers 404 {ok:false,out:'no such page: /settings'}; loadSettings then sets S.set to that error object, and settingsHTML reads d.tables of it, which is undefined, so the render throws and the view never loads. Fix: (1) loadSettings checks r.ok/j.tables, leaves S.set alone on an error and shows a plain message with Try again; (2) when the server has no such route, say the run is older than this page and must be restarted, not a raw 404; (3) look for the root fix: serve the page the run process started with (cache it at start) or otherwise keep page and server in step, without breaking a live run's page. Check: a test that loadSettings/settingsHTML on an ok:false reply renders the message and throws nothing, and one for the version skew. | files: agent_runner/web_page.html, agent_runner/web.py, tests/test_web.py — Same cause, worse: a person hit it repeatedly on 2026-11-23 with a Needs you card's Chat in iFizBox: 'Not sent: no such action: need_chat' in the Outbox (screenshot .runner/uploads/20261123-131621-image.png, R81). iFizBox's run (started 08:48) predates need_chat (F83, landed that noon) while its page is the new one from disk; Retry there can never work. So cover every new action, not only /settings: prefer the root fix (the page, web.py and ui_model the run started with stay in step; a run not restarted keeps serving its own page), and for any unknown action or route from an older run the page says 'this run is older than the page, restart it' with the restart control, and keeps the Outbox message to send after the restart. Check: need_chat against a server without it.

- [x] **F175** F175 first part: the ask file takes what a Needs you item needs from the person, and the model sends it (the run page's popup is F175a, the TUI's pane F175b, the agents' brief C37). The person, 2026-11-23: 'currently we don't ever use the wizard. we should instead make it like the needs you items show us simply what is needed on the webpage or tui'. The model reads from <notes>/asks/ID.json: link and cmd (with on, which machine) on a step, and inputs: [{key, label, kind text/link/secret, env, hint, match, confirm}]. match is a short regex: a failed match says the hint and sends nothing. confirm is one plain sentence asked before Done, for what publishes or cannot be undone, as F399's iCloud link needs. ui_model.need_give sends them: text and links go into the answer the task reopens with, quoted, length-limited, one line, never in a shell. A secret never goes into the plan, the run log, a transcript or the snapshot: it goes only to <notes>/asks/ID.env (owner-only, git-ignored, removed when the task lands or is dropped), and the note gives just the key's name and that path. A secret not sent over https or localhost is refused, saying why. An item with no inputs keeps F162's plain answers; a broken file falls back to them. Check: test_ui_model (fields read, broken file falls back, secret only in the env file and in none of plan, log, snapshot; the F399 example: steps add main's copy of Install Switch to Shortcuts, then Share > Copy iCloud Link; one link input with match and confirm). | files: agent_runner/need_detail.py, agent_runner/asks.py, agent_runner/ui_model.py, agent_runner/plan.py, docs/REFERENCE.md, GLOSSARY.md, tests/test_ui_model.py | after: F162
- [x] **F175a** F175a run page Needs you popup: draws and sends what F175 reads. An item detail carries steps (strings), step_more (one {} or {link, cmd, on} per step, [] when no step has any) and inputs ([{key, label, kind text/link/secret, env, hint, match, confirm}]). The popup draws the steps in order: link as an Open button, cmd as code with Copy and which machine (on); then one field per input (a secret is hidden as typed); then one Done button that sends them. An item with inputs draws no plain answers; an item with no inputs keeps F162's. Done sends to ui_model.need_give(tid, values, confirm, secure). need_give is not in ui_model.ACTIONS, so web.py routes it itself with the page's token and sets secure from the connection: https (also through Tailscale Serve or Runner Home) or localhost, never from what the page sends. Plain http: the secret field says why it takes none and need_give refuses. A result {'field': key, 'out': hint} shows at that field; one with confirm (code 3) is asked before Done sends again with confirm true. A refusal keeps what was typed; after sending, the field and its value are gone from the page; a secret is in no log, snapshot, localStorage, transcript or claude.ai copy (the copy draws no secret field). Example to test with: iFizBox F399 (today its ask is 'run the wizard'): steps add main's copy of Install Switch to Shortcuts, then Share > Copy iCloud Link; one link input with match and confirm. Check: test_web (popup draws and sends, http refuses a secret, confirm, match hint); screenshots at 390px and 1280px looked at. The person, 2026-11-23: 'make it like the needs you items show us simply what is needed on the webpage or tui'. | files: agent_runner/web.py, agent_runner/web_page.html, docs/STYLE.md, docs/REFERENCE.md, tests/test_web.py

Steps in order with their link (Open) and cmd with its machine, then one field per input (a secret hidden as typed), then one Done that calls ui_model.need_give(..., secure=True), since the TUI is local. match's hint shows at the field; confirm is asked first; a failed match sends nothing. Plain answers only for an item with no inputs. A secret is in no log or screen after sending. Open on your own how a link opens on this machine's TUI. - [x] **F401** Run page on touch: pull down past the top to refresh, as normal sites do (person, 2026-11-24). Why: the document is locked (html,body overscroll-behavior:none;overflow:hidden, body position:fixed, B160) and .body is overscroll-behavior:contain, so the browser's own pull-to-refresh can never fire, nor does an installed Home Screen app get one. Keep that lock. How: in 180-live.js's one-finger gesture code (SW), a touch that starts with every scroller under it at scrollTop 0 and moves mostly down becomes a pull: a small spinner at the top follows the finger with resistance, turns 'ready' past a threshold (about 70px), and letting go then calls newsRefresh() (it keeps typed text and holds the reload during recording, an upload or a secret); if it holds, or the pull was short or cancelled, the spinner springs back. Works in the main view (#body) and the sidebar list. Not: while a sheet, popup or concept view is open, in text boxes, in iframes (a chat's terminal keeps its own scroll), on a dragged row, with a second finger, or after a touch that has scrolled (B167: a touch is one thing until the finger lifts). Decide under 10px, preventDefault only once it is a pull. calm() gets no animation; the spinner has role=status; docs/STYLE.md's touch section gains the rule. Mouse and wheel unchanged. Check: node-driven tests beside the swipe ones: pull past threshold refreshes once; short pull, upward or sideways move, scrolled list, open sheet, text box and touchcancel do not; a drawer swipe and sheet swipe still work; a held reload springs back. | files: agent_runner/page/180-live.js, agent_runner/page/050-chats.logic.js, agent_runner/page/010-shared.css, docs/STYLE.md, tests/test_web_home.py

Start runs the command detached, so it outlives the run and the session, once only (a second Start is a no-op that says running); Stop ends the whole process group it started and nothing else (guard against a reused pid); state is running, stopped or failed with the last output lines, stored secrets hidden as in other device output. A page with no start command has no start/stop. CLI: run device start|stop NAME, and device list/show give the page and its state. - [x] **T31** The runner joins a conflict in the glossary file ([plan] glossary, GLOSSARY.md by default) by itself, in every project with no setting. Since 2026-11-23, 6 of iFizBox's 7 conflict fix sessions and 15 of agent-runner's 89 named it. Three-way join by term entry: terms added on either side are both kept under their own section, an entry changed or removed on one side takes that side; the same entry changed differently on both sides, or anything that does not parse, still goes to a fix session. Never write duplicate terms or lose an entry. A project's own [run.join] for that file wins. Check: tests for two added neighbouring terms, one-side edit and removal, the same term added differently on both sides (fix session), and unparseable text (fix session). Note it in CHANGELOG and docs/REFERENCE.md. | files: agent_runner/glossary.py, agent_runner/join.py, tests/test_glossary_join.py, docs/REFERENCE.md, CHANGELOG.md

'Use it anyway' sends holder=busy.key, which runs it for that holder only; a new holder asks again. Devices: op save's buttons list sets a device's buttons (absent keeps them, [] removes them).

- [x] **B227** (follow-up, normal) Auto restart is far too slow: the run restarts on every new version of its code on main (the person, 2026-11-24: 'fix auto restart, it only restarts when there's a new version so it can be very frequent'). Seen: B218a landed 12:57 and at 13:25 the page still served the 12:30 code, because merges kept landing: code_settled() waits for CODE_QUIET 600 s still, else CODE_LONGEST 3600 s, and watch_code() then waits for a tick with self.merging empty. Drop the batch wait (CODE_QUIET, CODE_LONGEST, code_settled): once main's agent_runner tree differs from the one started on, restart at the next moment with no merge thread. Overlapping merges can leave no such moment: hold new merge threads from starting while a restart is due (those under way finish, gates built ahead are adopted as today); the restarted run takes them up. - [x] **F451** Workers read less code. The person asked, 2026-11-25: run cost (7 days, 906 sessions) puts sed, grep and cat at 83% of the tool-result context, and 145 of 612 worker starts since 2026-11-20 passed the 150k context warning. F412 (2026-11-24) already gives the brief a map of big files: build on it, do not repeat it. (1) A task's brief tells its session to read only the ranges it needs (grep -n or the map first, then that range; no whole big file, no re-reading a range it has), and to hand a broad search over several files to a read-only search subagent (Explore) that returns file:line and a short conclusion; keep it to a few lines. (2) Leave the subagent line out in harness sessions and wherever there are no subagents (see subagents(), harness_session in brief.py). No cut to what a task may read when it needs it. - [x] **B845** Run page on a computer: words in a chat's live screen (session_frame's ttyd frame) can't be copied with Ctrl+C or right click. The person, 2026-11-25: 'when trying to ctrl c to copy in chat it doesnt work. right click doesnt work either'. Found in headless Chromium against ttyd 1.7.7 with a stand-in program: Claude Code's full screen turns mouse tracking on (seen on live sessions: mouse_any=1 sgr=1 alt=1), so a drag goes to the session as mouse reports and xterm selects nothing (getSelection() empty). Ctrl+C then reaches the session as \x03 (an interrupt, also with words selected: xterm sends it and clears the selection), and a right click is sent as a mouse report. ttyd 1.7.7's page has no OSC 52 handler, so what a session copies itself cannot reach the browser's clipboard that way. Shift+drag does select and copy (ttyd copies on selection). Fix, mouse only, in the frame's HEAD: a drag selects text as Shift+drag does, while a click without a drag still goes to the session; with words selected Ctrl+C (Cmd+C) copies them, keeps the session from getting \x03 and clears the selection; with none it goes to the session as now; a right click is never sent to the session and opens the browser's menu with Copy working on the selection. The wheel, touch, the session history's own selection and the login terminal keep working. Also check Ctrl+C and right click on words in the history (scrolled up) with focus last in the frame. Check: frame tests, and in a real Chromium with mouse tracking on (1000, 1002, 1006): drag, Ctrl+C and right click Copy each put the words on the clipboard, no \x03 in the program's input; a plain click still arrives; Ctrl+C with no selection arrives as \x03. | files: agent_runner/session_frame.py, tests/test_web_live.py | test: tests/test_web_live.py

- [x] **B841** A run that stops with landed work it could not push leaves it stranded: main holds it, origin has only task/<ID>, and nothing pushes it until another merge happens here. Seen with run once (F443, T26) while another device's runner kept landing: the push lost the race to origin/main each time and the run exited. Why: gitops.push leaves the shared branch to 'the next merge', which never comes for a run that ends (once, stop, idle shutdown), and another runner never merges this device's task branches. Fix: before a run exits (and on a run's start) with main ahead of the push remote, take origin in, run the gate and push, retrying while origin keeps moving; status and Needs you say when landed work is left unpushed. | files: agent_runner/gitops.py, agent_runner/land.py, agent_runner/loop.py

- [x] **B838** (follow-up, high) Run page, Status: an open page keeps showing old data until a reload by hand. The person, 2026-11-25: 'status page should update while viewing it, it keeps showing outdated and i have to refresh manually'. Measured that day: /events sent a new snapshot every 5-7 s, both straight and over https, so the likely cause is in the page (not reproduced: reproduce it first). connect() in 180-live.js redials only on onerror with readyState 2; a socket that died quietly (the phone slept, the network changed) stays OPEN, gives no error and no lost-connection bar, and nothing looks on visibilitychange, pageshow or online. The server's keep-alive is an SSE comment, which page code cannot see, so a dead stream looks like a quiet one. F206a fixed the same for a chat's session only. Wanted: the server's ping is an event the page can hear (named, with no data an open older page would take for a snapshot); with nothing heard for about 2 pings the page closes that stream, shows the lost-connection bar if the redial is not answered in 8 s, and dials again; shown again, back from the page cache or online, it dials at once unless something was heard in the last few seconds; one stream at a time, never two. A failed EventSource constructor's poll() stays. Also check that no exception in takeSnap() or render() is swallowed by onmessage's empty catch and leaves Status undrawn: log it. Check: logic test of when it redials; Chromium against a server that goes silent with the socket open: Status shows the new data with no reload, in under 40 s, and at once on becoming visible; one connection left open; Runner Home's proxy passes the ping. New version popup is F207, not this. | files: agent_runner/page/090-live.logic.js, agent_runner/page/180-live.js, agent_runner/web.py, tests/test_web_stream.py, docs/REFERENCE.md, CHANGELOG.md

- [x] **B885** (follow-up, high) Run page snapshot takes 2-7 s on Windows, so the page shows no project or status for seconds. Measured on work-laptop (agent-runner 0.78.0, cProfile of web.run_snapshot in a real project): 7 s per snapshot, of which ~6 s is ~55 git subprocesses per snapshot (CreateProcess ~25 ms + pipe reads ~100 ms each on Windows). Nearly all come from config.main_checkout() (77 calls per 3 snapshots, via Config.notes()) and config.toplevel() (86 calls): each spawns git rev-parse twice. Fix: memoize toplevel(path) and main_checkout(path) per resolved path within the process (a checkout's top and common dir never change; keep AGENT_RUNNER_REPO and cwd=None behaviour, give tests a way to clear the cache), and make Config.notes() not call main_checkout on every call (compute once per Config). Then look for other per-snapshot git spawns (status.events_file, need_detail.asks_file, merge_rows.live_gates/link_of, control.control_live_numbers) and cache them on the snapshot tick. Check: a test that counts subprocess.run calls during one ui_model snapshot and asserts it stays small (e.g. <= 5) and that a second snapshot spawns no git for main_checkout/toplevel; existing tests pass. | files: agent_runner/config.py, agent_runner/ui_model_snapshot.py, agent_runner/status.py, agent_runner/need_detail.py, agent_runner/merge_rows.py, tests/test_snapshot_speed.py

- [x] **B31** (follow-up, normal) A parked task retries without limit across runs (R5 audit, docs/FACTORY.md). _finish (runner.py) parks a branch that failed its merge three times as wip/<ID>, wip/<ID>-2, … and leaves the task open; tried is local to run(), so every new run, and every auto_upgrade restart (restart_if_upgraded execs a fresh process), starts it again. Nothing counts the attempts, and 'not merged, parked as' is not in NEEDS_YOU, so no person hears of it. Fix: count parked attempts per task (the wip/<ID>* branches, or a note) and after 2 block the task with BLOCKED: and the ledger reasons instead of starting it again; add the parked event to the needs-you feed. - [x] **B39** (follow-up, low) A task text containing ' BLOCKED: ' loses its files (found in R5). fields() (plan.py) reads keyed fields only from the part of the line before ' BLOCKED: ', since block() appends the marker there; an open task whose prose mentions the marker (say, 'block it with BLOCKED: and the reasons') has its | files: after it, so plan add warns 'no files', files_on_line returns nothing and pick runs it beside anything. R5's B31 hit it and was dropped. Fix: split on the last ' BLOCKED: ' that follows the fields, or have plan add refuse or rewrite the marker in its text. Done when plan add of such a text keeps its files | files: agent_runner/plan.py, tests/test_plan.py — dropped: its own text hid its files (the bug it describes); refiled
- [x] **B40** (follow-up, low) A task text that contains the block marker (space, BLOCKED, colon, space) loses its files (found in R5). fields() (plan.py) reads keyed fields only from the part of the line before that marker, since block() appends it there; an open task whose prose quotes the marker has its files field after it, so plan add warns no files, files_on_line returns nothing and pick runs it beside anything. A busy loop burns usage until the session gives up. - [x] **B37** (follow-up, normal) The sequential run pushes the shared branch after any commit, gated or not (R5 audit, docs/FACTORY.md). sequential() (runner.py) pushes the checked-out branch with shared=True whenever HEAD moved, without checking that done's gate passed on that tree: a session that ran done --no-test, committed after done, or committed and never ran done gets pushed untested. The parallel run guards this with gated() and refs/agent-runner/tested/<ID>. Not time zone (start is made zone-aware) and not the missing origin (default_branch falls back to the current branch). - [x] **B33** (follow-up, normal) Follow-ups filed by parallel slots are deduped only against the slot's own copy of the plan (R5 audit, docs/FACTORY.md). With the plan in the repo (the default), plan add in a worktree checks similar() (plan.py) against that branch's TODO.md, which lacks what other slots filed and have not merged; the merge-plan driver then keeps both lines and renumber_doubles (runner.py) only gives the second a new id. Two slots that hit the same flaky test or missing helper file the same follow-up twice, and two sessions then do it. Fix: before a merge, run similar() for each task the branch added against the shared branch's open tasks and, on a strong match, note it on the existing task instead of adding it (log it). Fix: when [run] test is empty, log a loud warning at run start and in dry-run and status (no gate: merges go to main untested), and have a parallel run refuse to push main without [git] push_merge set explicitly. - [x] **B158** (follow-up, normal) Run page: a message typed into a control session whose TUI is not up is lost though send_fifo says ok. Reproduced in B154 on 2026-11-23 with the real Claude Code 2.1.330 through run_pty: typed 0 or 0.8 s into a 1.5 s launcher (sh sleep then exec claude, as `run control N` does before it execs claude), the text shows in the prompt box but is never submitted (not in the transcript after 25 s); probably the cooked terminal turns the Enter into a newline. Typed at 5 s it lands in 1 s. The run page hits it with messages queued for '@cid', sent the moment new_control(wait=False) names the control session. Fix: hold the message until the session's log shows ESC[?2004h (bracketed paste on) since its job started and its own first message (type_first_later) is in, then send in order. - [x] **B222a** (follow-up, normal) Run page: typing in a Needs you popup's reply box breaks autocorrect and replaces characters (the person, 2026-11-24). Cause, from reading the code, not reproduced on a phone: needSheet() in 070-plan.js writes the typed words into the markup (value=S.f[f]), so after typing the next refresh (sheetDraw, setHTML) replaces the whole sheet, input included, under the keyboard; keepFields() restores words and caret but autocorrect state is lost. The minute ticking in n.age does the same. Fix: no typed value in that markup (keepFields() puts it back, as for give fields), and a refresh that changes other parts (age, notes, steps) leaves the focused reply or give input element in place: patch only what changed, do not hold refreshes for a fixed time. Check: tests that the same input node stays through a refresh after typing and after an age change; words return after closing and reopening; an answered item still closes the popup. Try an iOS-like browser if available, else say so in the commit. | files: agent_runner/page/070-plan.js, agent_runner/page/100-shared.js, tests/test_web.py

Cause: a switch loads /p/KEY/, 456 KB uncompressed with no-store; at 8 Mbit/s, 100 ms first paint is 0.6 to 0.75 s, unthrottled 45 ms; the server answers in 12 ms. Send it gzipped when accepted (151 KB), compressed once per page version and project; give it an ETag and 'private, no-cache' for a 304 on a switch back, only after the token check; everything else keeps no-store. It holds through Home's proxy and on Home's own pages. - [x] **B787** (follow-up, normal) Run page chats, iPhone: scrolling up in a chat draws the history's text near-black on the dark ground, unreadable. The person, 2026-11-24: 'runner chat is buggin when scrolling'; screenshot /home/ryha/repos/agent-runner/.runner/uploads/20261124-195743-IMG_2375.png (its plain text is #141413, the light look's text, on #262624; links, muted text and bullets are right). Cause found in pastLook (092-history.js, F191b): the cache key has css.length, not the css. The light and dark sheets can be the same length, so when pastLook runs while the page already says dark but the terminal is still light, the stale colour it wrote inline on #pastrows stays for good while #pastcss is updated. Shown in node with the real pastLook and stub sheets: sheet #f3f1ea, inline #141413; with css in the key both are #f3f1ea. What set it off on the phone is not reproduced: likely a light/dark flip (iOS does one when the app goes to the background) with pastLook run before lookAgain. Fix: (1) the key compares the css itself; (2) a look change while the history is drawn (HX.st armed or up) draws it in the new colours too, its cloned screen rows included (xterm puts contrast colours inline on them), keeping what stands on screen where it is. Check: tests/test_web_history.py runs the real pastLook (it is stubbed there now) with two equal-length sheets and a change of computed colour; in a browser, chromium or webkit, flip light/dark at rest, then scrolled up, and read the text colour. | files: agent_runner/page/092-history.js, agent_runner/page/090-chats.js, tests/test_web_history.py

The person OK'd queueing this (control session 12's question, F431, 2026-11-25). Cause, from s1's transcript: s1's agent on C41b ran a script of plan edit ID --files on 44 tasks, ran it again at 11:36:21, then a corrected file-list pass over 34 at 11:36:44. control_settled.noting records one event per successful command, except an edit that is only --pri; step types each event separately. Fix: an edit that only changes --files, like one that only changes --pri (or both together), records nothing, as it settles nothing. Several waiting or recent events with the same task, same what, same author and same reason become one settled line per session; the builder chooses the window, long enough for the 42 s above. A different author, verb or reason is still told, and an edit of the text is still told. Out of scope: when an unmerged worktree's edit should be told; note it only.
